ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-22-S1-commons.u6	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, because the debate on social benefits and benefits has prevailed in the public debate in recent years, I will first of all refer to three propaganda lies and a paradox. The first lie, repeated by Mr. Mitsotakis in his Programming Declarations, is that the benefits concern poverty. The Prime Minister is well aware that child benefits - a child's allowance, nursery stations, school meals - amounting to 1,400,000, the allowance for the disabled of EUR 800 million, the home allowance of EUR 350 million not only for the poor but also the middle layers, namely the many. The second falsehood is that for SYRIZA to give benefits and benefits, he exaggerated the middle class by impoverishing it. On the contrary, ELSTAT's recent figures - June 2019, now - showed that the PASOK and New Democracy governments have poored the middle class, since by 2015 one out of six middle-grade citizens went to the poor, while the rest lost 37% of their income. If Mr Mitsotakis reduces the budget for allowances, he will again reduce the incomes of the middle class. The third lie is that benefits are deducted from development resources. It has, however, proved to be developmental, it is money that does not go to offshore, increase consumption, create new jobs. We increased the funds for welfare from EUR 780 million to EUR 3.3 billion in 2019. This account is under threat of being cut to cover the budget gap of 1.8 billion, as we have estimated, created by the Prime Minister's announcements. The only poverty benefit is the CRL, the Social Income of Solidarity. In 2015, in conditions of humanitarian crisis, there was no budget for poverty for seven hundred thousand people who were in extreme poverty and impoverished conditions. The minimum guaranteed income, which Mr Brutsis spoke about yesterday as his own achievement, was a pilot project for 2014, involved thirteen municipalities from the three hundred twenty-five, had a budget of EUR 16 million and was considered ineffective and inadequate in his political implementation. We reviewed it. Therefore, with the humanitarian crisis programme, a solidarity card etc., the CRL and the measures for the child have been reduced in two and a half years - look at ELSTAT's elements - poverty and child poverty before the crisis. See exactly where we reached evidence, that is, 2018. In other words, in simple words, the beneficiaries of the CRL have been reduced because we had had to anticipate their integration into work. Here is the paradox: The New Democracy that does not want the benefits because they recycle poverty, announced that it will increase the number of beneficiaries of the CRL. This can only mean two things in my sense: or he knows that poverty will increase with her policy or is the IMF's well-known recipe, which told us in the negotiation, "You will streamline..." - notice the word - "... the benefits, you will save 1 billion, you will give this money the minimum guaranteed income and that is where you will include all the allowances". For the extreme poor, that is. The terminology of'support criteria', used by Mrs Michailidou recently, is the same that she used by the IMF when demanding benefits cuts. So the problem is not whether you will abolish the four basic allowances, where yesterday Mr Brutsi assured us that he will not be abolished. The problem is if you change the criteria for beneficiaries, so if you reduce the account. We have created a new, exemplary digital welfare and social solidarity state with two digital pillars: on the one hand, the regions and the two forty community centres, on the other hand, the OPECA, which delivers all digital benefits, crosses six databases in one second, without suspicious mediation, abolishing customer relations and corruption. Thus citizens enjoy their rights with dignity. You voted against this. We had a state institution on school lunches. Two hundred thousand children eat every day a hot meal based on the Mediterranean diet against obesity. We had a plan for all primary school, six hundred thousand kids eating each day for free by the end of 2021. You voted against them. Will you remove them? We gave to all the mothers - workers and unemployed - voucher free of charge for their children at nursery stations, that is, a hundred forty thousand vouchers from seventy-nine thousand received in 2015. Question: The right of the unemployed mother to take voucher will you keep it? The disabled get their allowance every day every month. The allowance is inconsistent and tax-free, while 15% of the public's jobs are exclusively for them. Dehydration, roofs of supported living, family support centres, third-age family, was established, while the law of assumption and adoption reduced the waiting time from six to eight years that were in eight to twelve months and braked into infant trade. We are proud of what we have delivered and achieved in four very difficult years and we will defend them, because unfortunately the first ten days of the government are particularly worrying. Firstly, they brought the lifting of the Roma Special Secretariat that had launched the relocation and integration of the Roma, who are staying in impoverished camps, work, education and health with significant European funds that we have earned. Secondly, you have brought about the abolition of the Special Secretariat for Social and Solidarity Economics, which had prepared a programme of aid for a thousand three hundred ESDPs with 47 million. Thirdly, you transferred responsibility for unaccompanied applicants from the General Secretariat of Welfare and Social Solidarity to the Ministry of Civil Protection, not to mention the fact that they are children and their care belongs to the Child Protection and not to the Police.

They are growth tools, they give thousands of jobs, but also in the long term, if you want, they help whole regions to grow, upgrade and escape isolation. In particular, in the current state of our country, a crucial factor in overcoming the crisis is a balanced infrastructure programme. Of course, we all know that the resources today are extremely limited. We all know in this country that there is no money. We therefore need to use the PPP very carefully, but also private funds. We are in front of very important decisions. We don't look good on the situation. We did not do so by election, and we will obviously not do so today either. You will allow me here to say some truths that I think the National Representation should listen to. Firstly, we need absolute respect for public money, transparency and efficiency. We are here to ensure the public interest, the interests of all, not of the few. The Prime Minister, in an election, presented the New Democracy infrastructure programme, and then we started with a basic finding: The model of major discounts or, as the technical world says, the unusually low bids, eventually ends up paying public projects much more expensive and doing so in twice as much time as three times as a year. On the contrary, the concessions, i.e. the private sector's involvement with these large projects, have taught us that this way of implementing the projects is more effective, at less cost than classical public works. I will give you an example, which I think is a characteristic: the Raptomatis tunnel in Tripoli, a two-way tunnel and exactly the same construction conditions. One was made under a concession agreement, i.e. with the private sector within two years. The second was done as a classic public work in fourteen years and at twice the cost. Today I am going to dwell on three projects that have so far been, in our view, an example for avoidance. Firstly, Patras - Tower, a project that was ready for funding five years ago and today would be complete, we received after the decision of the SYRIZA government in 2015 split into eight pieces. Three of the eight projects have not even been signed. One contractor, who had won the four contracts, is all aware that he has huge problems at the moment. The remaining five projects have not even begun to implement the contracts and only one has been paid in advance. The partitions of works almost always lead to rackets or huge delays and the Greek people pays them three times over. Excessive discounts put the project at risk. The culmination, ladies and gentlemen? The previous government was supposed to have launched an election project. But do you know what we received in the fifth contractor? A dump with fifty thousand tonnes of garbage and the contractor's demand to get two million euros to clean up the garbage from the contractor. As it is today - this is what the entire technical world knows - we are at a dead end. It's out of the question to discuss whether it'll be delayed or lost money. The work is literally in the air and needs a radical solution. We are studying the alternatives to the Ministry in detail. We are in constant contact with the European Commission, so that we can reach a solution as quickly as possible and with guaranteed funding. We are committed to doing this and to doing it. Second project: Aktio - Ambrosia. To date it has cost - listen -6.1m euros per kilometre without studies and project supervision. It started in 2010 with four budget contractors EUR 142 million. In 2017, a contractor - broom - was auctioned to complete the budget project - pay attention to -160m euros, that is, more expensive than the original budget, more than four projects together. But we all know that the project is in the air, because we have judicial involvement and it was auctioned in the wrong way. This is how supposedly large discounts work. That's how the partitions work. Projects are delaying, getting out of every timeframe and are never properly structured. So we are also called upon to review the tender, to give the best solution here to complete this work at last, so that we can finish with the great revolutionary words that eventually bring ten years of delays in major projects, additional conventions which are the joy of the contractor and economic excesses. Thirdly, with regard to BOAK, you say that it has been auctioned. The truth is, an idea and a sketches have been auctioned. As it is planned, everyone knows in Crete that the project is not going to start, because we are talking about three individual projects, with three different ways of tendering, in fact about a partition, without studies and most of all, without environmental approval. What are we planning on doing? Initially, this bizarre architecture of the project needs to be considered and changed. Let's finally start the studies. We can't auction works without having studies. This is not happening anywhere in the world. Empty the environmental work. We will not just activate the OPEC again, which unfortunately the previous Minister for Infrastructure has put on the shelf, but we will substantially strengthen it and then start their tender procedures. Of course, we have other major issues in the Ministry these days, such as the Attica road issue, which employs millions of users. After negotiations with the previous government, the consortium announced increases in tolls. This was because these negotiations obviously did not end up anywhere. So the Attica Road announced an increase in tolls within the election period. You'll tell me, did he have a contractual right to do so? Yeah. Is she right? Of course not, because he did not respect the citizens who are users of this road in particular. We will not sit, ladies and gentlemen, with our hands crossed. One thing is certain, and we must stress this: the new government will not be able to accept it in its entirety, as it has many ways of reacting. In addition to this, we plan to move quickly on to projects that are in progress or are stuck. Of course, as we have already mentioned many times, the most important thing is to prepare a strategic plan for the new generation of projects that will boost the market and change the image of the country in the years to come, because we cannot have this phenomenon that we have in Greece, whenever the government changes, every time the Minister changes everything. We refer to projects that will be fully documented. There will be feasibility and sustainability studies, and there will, in any case, be what foreigners say, a simple feasibility plan. In this context, in our programming we have to solve the huge problems in Kastelli and finally start this project. Let us move quickly on to projects which are already being implemented, such as in the southern part of E65. To proceed with the competitive procedures on Line 4 of Metro, Alsos Beikos - Goudi, despite the many problems that exist, as well as on the Athens and Thessaloniki Metro projects, with a definitive solution for the Egnatia and Agia Sophia stations. And this will be done in full cooperation with the Ministry of Culture. To proceed with the competition of the Salamina submarine road link with Perama, which has been delayed many years. To proceed with the implementation of a series of projects that we have already announced, such as Elefsina - Material, the extensions of the Ionian Road with Agrini and Cakavia, Drama - Amphipolis - Egnatia. To complete the design and implementation of a national rail network in Athens - Thessaloniki, a border in Athens - Patras, because we must also attach great importance to the maintenance of the national rail network. The state must do everything it can to prevent accidents and not run after them. However, we attach particular importance to projects for the safety of citizens. Within two weeks the government and the Ministry directly addressed the natural disasters in Halkidiki, as well as the recent earthquake in Athens. The Ministry of Infrastructure has responsibility for what projects have been designated as a national level, at the request of the regional bodies. However, we are also paying particular attention to institutional amendments. We said it as opposition, and we say it now: we don't care to take everything out. We won't change Law 4412 and Law 4413. We will improve them with small, but significant changes that apply to all the countries of the European Union, such as the possibility of supervising public projects where private companies need to be supervised, feasibility and sustainability studies for large projects and, furthermore, we will consider the possibility of tendering for innovative projects. We are interested in the essential innovations, the new modern financial instruments. We must finally make use of ideas and solutions tried abroad, because in this country we must finally stop discovering the wheel. We attach great importance to the preparation of studies which may also be controlled by individuals. That's how we're gonna save a lot of time. We are aiming to create an expropriation observatory where criteria, prices, conditions, process and monitoring of all expropriations in the country are gathered. At the same time, we will implement something that is happening everywhere in Europe, the project register, which is also a proposal from the TEU. The State will be able to know, for example, the projects of competence to each body per municipality, per district or public company and who is responsible for their maintenance. ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-22-S1-commons.u51	Ladies and gentlemen, first of all I would like to thank the Prime Minister, Mr Mitsotakis, for the honour he has made me choose as the Secretary of State for Development and Investment in today's government, with basic responsibilities for the Programme for Public Investment and the ESDP. It is also a great honour today to address the Plenary Assembly of the Greeks. I may disappoint you that my word may not be verbally correct and has no political time, but I promise you that it will be sincere and with supply and work. In a country where the crisis has hit the Gross Domestic Product, as well as the human potential it produces, the objective of convergence with the developed regions of the European Union may be more timely than ever. Our policy focuses on broadening the country's development potential, speeding up the pace of economic growth, increasing productivity to achieve real convergence and, of course, improving the quality of life of citizens. At the same time, however, we are aiming to alleviate the socio-economic disparities that still exist. We are aiming at an economy that is competitive in a society of cohesion and prosperity. We also aim to speed up the investment that serves national policies in each sector, industry, energy, the environment, small and medium-sized enterprises, as well as the rapid and correct performance of projects and actions in society, from large-scale infrastructure and networks to financing for small and medium-sized enterprises, from the financial tools for information technology up to the strengthening of good governance and the upgrading of public sector services. All this touches our everyday lives. We will do all of this with good management, with credibility in Europe, but also with accountability and responsibility for the Greek citizens. In this way we contribute to convergence, rebuild the national economy, strengthen social justice, limiting existing inequalities, and ultimately give the Greeks the prosperity they expect and really deserve. The ten-year crisis in Greece has had a negative impact on two key and critical parameters. It has dramatically reduced the investment of enterprises in fixed equipment, as well as the investment of firms in research and development. These two led to the creation of a very large deficit of competitiveness of Greek businesses. At the same time, foreign direct investment has moved to a low average of 6%, coupled with the low public investment programme and also the low absorption of the NRP, it has further contributed to the expansion of this competitiveness deficit on the market. At the same time, however, we must understand that the environment and market structure are changing rapidly, with new trends and branches emerging and, as many previous speakers have said, the fourth industrial revolution, with basic features on the Internet of things, big data, FinTech, the blockchain chain that is a common public accounting book, where now too many functions are based on it. Nor should we forget climate change, in which market and entrepreneurship, at least abroad, have already started to adapt. The Ministry of Development and Investment will design and implement actions and programmes with measurable objectives, which will take into account the above and also other important features such as the cyclical economy, sustainability, etc., so that growth is long-term, with strong foundations and is not affected by at least short-term crises. More specifically, the actions we will focus will be the rationalisation of the Public Investment Programme, its long-term programming and its maximum leverage with private funds primarily from foreign investments. Priority will be given to projects of added economic and social value, in order to make effective use of the last available Community resource, to promote the necessary interventions for effective coordination of both the national and the co-financed part of the Public Investment Programme. In this context, it will immediately be modernised. The existing legal framework dates back to 1952. At the same time, it is essential to optimise the existing NRP for its maximum absorption and, as I said before, with sustainable projects that have a direct impact on growth, but also in the long term, increase employment. The main objective is to mitigate the investment losses during the crisis and to transfer the Greek economy to a model of entrepreneurship, in which the export of invasion and extroversion will be a priority. This will also be done with the rationalisation of existing ESDP financial programmes. There is at the moment too much low absorption. We are looking forward to very few with extended selection criteria. Priority will be given to financial instruments, as these are recyclable, and when they are finished, the returning funds turn as national funds into the Public Investment Programme. Co-financed loan products with favourable interest rates and low collateral, venture capital and loan guarantees for small and medium-sized enterprises will be the head of our interventions. In other words, we will increase significantly what we call 'false' business financing. The final introduction of the microfinance operating framework will also be important, which is done on all European markets. At the same time, we are laying the foundations for the timely and efficient use of the resources for the next 2021-2027 programme of the new ESDP, through a substantial and representative consultation with the social partners and regions, so that the implementation of the programmes begins on the first day of their start. We will strengthen liquidity in the real economy by making use of the European financial instruments of 'COSME', 'InnovFin', 'InvestEU', which, together with the proper implementation of the Public Investment Programme and the ESRB, will give the necessary access to capital of Greek enterprises. A sound and continuous coordination with the other ministries, regions and municipalities for a proper series of priority projects, ensuring the completion of the project, and certainly ensuring the absorption of all European resources. Create technical assistance tanks from the NSRF and other sources that will be used to speed up investment and infrastructure and enhance the competitiveness of small and medium-sized enterprises. There is no doubt that recovery has begun and that is the lawfulness of the market, but until now this is an unfunded recovery in economists' terminology, and it must be done as soon as possible in the right direction and on solid foundations. The issue of the new seven-year bond last week and the interest rate that was formed shows that the markets believe in the future of Greece and the governance I have the honour of being a member. Thank you for listening.

ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-22-S1-commons.u88	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, with a sense of great honour and responsibility, I am here today to develop our priorities and the main actions that we will take in the next period in the field of education. On the occasion of this first speech, I would like to thank publicly the Prime Minister for the excellent honour of entrusting me with this crucial portfolio for the present and future of our children and our country. Let me begin by mentioning the six main objectives that are horizontally defined in our programme. Objective one: greater freedom, greater autonomy for schools and universities. We are exploiting the great potential of our teachers, we are liberating their dynamism. Second objective: extroversion and more substantial interconnection with the labour market. Third objective: equal opportunities for all. Education is the most effective mechanism for social mobility and our main concern is to ensure equal access to quality education for all. Fourth objective: emphasis and utilization of new data from the digital age. At a time when technology is changing rapidly and radically our lives and the real economy, it is not about access to knowledge, it is about more efficient processing and utilization of knowledge. Development of technology at all levels of education, cultivation of the necessary digital skills in our young people. Fifth objective: it is not only to deliver to society educated young people but also more integrated citizens, citizens who have developed mild skills, who can adapt to an environment that is constantly changing, who will have advanced indicators of cooperation, mutual respect and reciprocity. The aim is therefore to strengthen the indicators of social capital, which will boost social and economic development. Adopted by the Commission on 16 December. In order to improve the educational processes we should be able to measure them and value them. It is therefore necessary to place greater emphasis on the finances of education, namely the study of all the economic aspects of the educational process, with an emphasis on human capital, so that we have the appropriate tools to design and implement more efficiently. Ladies and gentlemen, in the last four and a half years education has been affected by the logic of a minor effort, equilibrium downwards, the demonization of excellence. This course will be restrained. The values of hard work, dignity and excellence will be restored. And since I am talking about excellence, ladies and gentlemen, for us excellence is not a relic, for us excellence is an attitude of life. Excellency is constantly trying to get over yourself, trying to get better every day. In particular, with regard to our programming positions, starting from the foundations, from the bottom of the pyramid upwards, we will place particular emphasis on strengthening pre-school education and we are already working hard to make two-year pre-school education possible, despite the significant shortages in infrastructure that we have inherited from the outgoing government. Continuing primary and secondary education, our central aim is to free the school from the suffocating embrace of the Ministry of Education and to show greater confidence in teachers. We are simplifying a series of procedures for the organisation of educational activities, such as the conduct of school tours and the participation in competitions and Olympics. We embellish the school unit, upgrade the role of the director, promote a new system of selection of training managers, reorganise the structures of support for the educational project, focusing on structures closer to the physical school unit, gradually abolishing existing hydro-heads and centralised support structures, and give more freedom to teachers in terms of teaching in order. We are investing more in teachers means, ladies and gentlemen, and that we will provide them with the appropriate tools so that they can carry out their work in an uninterrupted manner. We focus, after many years, on the training of teachers. We are promoting an evaluation system starting from school units and then moving towards teachers, with the sole aim of rewarding teachers and improving them through training. We record specific needs for general education teachers across the country and gradually resolve the major issue of assistant teachers. And we are announcing today, ladies and gentlemen, the reduction in the secondment of teachers to institutions to return more teachers to classes. In special treatment, we are already speeding up the recruitment process of four and a half thousand teachers. On a third level, we ensure access to quality education for all students and equip our young people with new skills. We are reintroducing and strengthening the institution of pilot and experimental schools. We are studying a new coherent curriculum from primary to high school. The curriculum will also be enriched with new educational themes, such as volunteering, entrepreneurship, respect for others, prevention of natural disasters, nutrition and hygiene. We are strengthening the learning of foreign languages and informatics knowledge. We emphasise the cultivation of so-called'soft skills' and so-called 'digital skills'. We establish school orientation from high school so that children are able to discover their actual gradients early. We are gradually adapting our educational system towards the full development of inclusive education. We are setting up training programmes for the school structures of special education and training appropriately weighted for the type of disability and special educational needs and we are setting up protected apprenticeship and training workshops. We are strengthening the autonomous educational role of high school, with targeted interventions in relation to the national education centre and the promotional examinations from classroom to class. We are establishing a new framework for private education, with greater degrees of freedom, for example in the preparation of additional educational activities and at the same time protecting the working rights of teachers. We are immediately improving distortions of the current system of introduction into higher education and, over time, without surprise to the educational world, we are bringing about more substantial changes, after thorough monitoring with the stakeholders concerned. We set a minimum basis for introduction to higher institutions, and from there on, institutions may define an import base which they choose is higher than the minimum import base. As also the institutions, as the Prime Minister has already announced, will set the annual number of importers. We are radically changing vocational education and Lifelong Learning. Our aim is for vocational training from a need-solving solution to become a conscious choice and employment tool for many. For example, we set up vocational schools standards and provide for a more active role for the social partners at local and regional level. We are setting up a new framework for the operation of vocational apprenticeship schools. In Lifelong Learning, it is possible to certify knowledge and skills to all interested parties within the framework of non-formal and informal learning. We upgrade adults' skills for optimal integration or reintegration into the labour market and reduce social exclusion. Ladies and gentlemen, I come to Higher Education as well. The reform in Higher Education aims at academic institutions that are truly autonomous, self-governing, evaluated, competitive, extroverted, in common stepping up with the labour market. Indicatively, as you know and as has already been announced, we are returning the universities to those who really belong, namely students, teachers and administrative staff, by abolishing the wrongly understood asylum. Public authorities will be able to intervene on their own initiative for any criminal offence that takes place within the institutions. Secondly, we are establishing modern and efficient administrative structures, with distinct and clearly defined responsibilities. Thirdly, we are establishing a maximum time limit for the completion of undergraduate studies, n+2, with the exception of exceptions, mainly for health reasons. Fourthly, we are strengthening the autonomy and extroversion of the institutions. We are liberalising postgraduate courses. We are widening the possibility of creating foreign-language undergraduate programmes. We support summer and winter schools in areas where the country has a comparative advantage, such as culture, such as shipping, such as tourism. We develop distance education programmes at undergraduate and postgraduate level, with investment in new technologies. We are making use of the possibility of public and private partnerships to meet the fundamental needs of the institutions, such as the construction of student restaurants, which our country so much needs, through so-called PPPs. We are introducing a new institutional framework for donations to institutions and for the implementation of patents and scientific ideas. We are moving towards simplifying the working framework of the PPE, without violating the rules on transparency and accountability. We extend the institution of student practice to public and private bodies in relation to their studies. Fifthly, we applaud the principle of quality assurance and certification in Higher Education, the so-called IPI, and we introduce evaluation procedures at all levels. State funding to the Higher Institutions will depend on objective criteria such as the cost of study per student, the duration of study programmes, the size and geographical spread of the institution, but, ladies and gentlemen, state funding will be linked to some of the results of the evaluation of these institutions. The country's academic map will be derived from the assessment of the PPP and on the basis of specific criteria, in order not to repeat the catastrophic phenomenon of the establishment of new sections and mergers in order to serve purely electoral designs and micropolitical interests. Finally, regarding the area of religions, the opportunity has recently been given to both the Prime Minister and me to develop some initial thoughts on the new thought that we intend to build with the Church, respecting the distinct institutional roles of State and Church. Ladies and gentlemen, some of our programme positions on education require money to be implemented and, yes, we will seek to increase the state budget for education. But most of our interventions do not require money. They just demand political will and a change of attitude. Greece of tomorrow is Greece who first invests in its children, designs for them, envisions with boldness, improves processes and infrastructures and shapes the conditions for an even better future than ours. We believe deep, ladies and gentlemen, that our future passes through our schools and universities. It's up to us tomorrow. Knowing this responsibility, believing in the new children and the possibilities of the excellent teachers of education, we will build the new free school and university. This is our mission. And in this effort we are and will be open to every proposal, to every idea, to every dialogue. We'll work hard to give our children the future they deserve. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-22-S1-commons.u125	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, first of all I wish everyone a good and fertile parliamentary term. In the elections of 7 July 2019, the Greek people gave a clear and strong mandate to the New Democracy and Prime Minister, Kyriakos Mitsotakis, to work and build a strong Greece, Greece of regularity, Greece of stability, Greece of the rule of law, Greece of growth, Greece of prosperity. Our task is to bring our country back to the place it deserves so that it recovers its international authority and consolidates it in the geopolitical environment as a credible and powerful force that promotes security and cooperation in the wider region. After four and a half years of SYRIZA and SYRIZA governance - ANEL the bet on returning to normality is not small, it is great. The faith, however, is being won by the strong political will and a genuine, deeply democratic patriotism that I can assure you that it characterises both the Government and the New Democracy Parliamentary Group. Having fully realised this critical national mission, I am taking responsibility for the political leadership of the country's Armed Forces entrusted to me by the Prime Minister of the country. The honour is extremely great and I assure the House of the Greek Parliament that both I and the Deputy Minister Alkiviadis Stefanis, who is present, are fully aware of the sacred debt that we are undertaking at this time, so that the Armed Forces of the country do their job in full in order for each Greek and every Greek to feel safe. Ladies and gentlemen, our country is in a dynamic political and geo-economic environment, the one of the south-eastern Europe and the eastern Mediterranean in which almost all the modern security challenges are encountered. In this complex and fluid environment, the wider security challenges encountered both globally and at regional level, potentially affecting our country as well, are also added. Unintentional and voluntary movements of large-scale populations, illegal immigration, terrorist activities and the exacerbation of extreme religious fundamentalism, illegal movements of all kinds, but especially illegal movements of people, unresolved so far but in any case extremely diverse Middle East, the strong competition for the exploitation of wealthy sources, particularly hydrocarbons, climate change that has recently begun to experience with vigour and our country, organised and internationalised crime, cyber threats are only some of the modern challenges that require our full vigilance, since they can adversely affect the security of our country. And since I am talking about challenges I inevitably come in our relationship with neighbour Turkey and developments in the south-eastern Mediterranean. It goes without saying, ladies and gentlemen, that we cannot but will not tolerate violations which directly challenge territorial integrity, our sovereign rights and the responsibilities arising from international conventions and decisions by international organisations. The Ministry of National Defence closely monitors Turkey's actions and behaviour in the wider region of the Eastern Mediterranean, a behaviour that seriously concerns us, as it also concerns the countries of the European Union, but also other countries that monitor Turkey's illegal and abusive actions within the exclusive Economic Zone of the Republic of Cyprus, namely a Member State of the European Union. We, Greece, stand with absolute commitment on the part of Cyprus, seek peace and good neighbourly relations with a view to respecting national sovereign rights and international law. We don't challenge it, but we don't like it and we don't like it. We are not restless, but we are vigilant. Our temper and orientation for peaceful resolution of disputes between states must not be seen as weakness. We believe that the creation and escalation of tensions is not in the interests of either of the two countries, nor, of course, is it helping to promote security and cooperation in an already quite agitated area. We would obviously like our relationship with Turkey to enter a new, another phase in which stability in our region is ensured, but that does not depend on us alone. As the Ministry of Defence we will seek to maintain a channel of communication with Turkey, notably by implementing activities in the context of the implementation of confidence-building measures. We really hope that Turkey will also seek to do so. We expect practical evidence of its intentions, but I am very afraid that so far we do not have them. Ladies and gentlemen, the Ministry of National Defence structures its political priorities on five main axes which serve the strategic choice of Prime Minister and Government for security in the country. Axle number one: Ensuring defensibility of defensive equipment. Particular attention will be paid to its technological leadership and to the use of modern information systems and administrative systems. Our aim is to implement targeted armaments programmes already identified by the leadership of the Armed Forces and evolve on the basis of short-, medium- and long-term priorities. This overall plan will first ensure that the country's Armed Forces are maintained and progressively strengthened and structured at two levels. The first level is about upgrading and supporting basic weapons systems in the Army, Navy and Air Force, according to the needs, design and priorities that we have set together with the military leadership. The second level concerns the acquisition of weapon systems that will upgrade the operational capacity and operational readiness of all three arms of the Armed Forces. This is where we are dealing with a level more specific and more direct intervention. Axis two: Reorganisation of the structure of the Armed Forces. The aim is to increase the availability of the spiritual potential by strengthening the necessary military personnel, as well as the establishment of an objective, stable career development framework and evaluation system, in order to ensure a worthwhile hierarchical development of executives. Third axis: Modernisation of the existing legislative framework. The main intervention will be the revision of the Procurement Act to the Armed Forces - all the legislative framework - to ensure, on the one hand, all the necessary transparency, without pseudo-transparency impressions and the necessary flexibility on the other in the system of supplies, across the range of the Armed Forces. Axis 4: Strengthening the domestic defence industry as vital for security of supply and the maintenance of operational capabilities of the Armed Forces. This assistance will be provided through the encouragement of defence industry partnerships with the private sector, support for defence research and technology in cooperation with the national universities and research institutes. It is a axis, I would say, of key, strategic importance and perspective for the country. Fifth axis: upgrading of military training. I am referring both to actions to exploit military service to obtain decisive qualifications, even to the entry into the labour market by soldiers and to the development of critical skills for this purpose, and to the strengthening of cooperation between senior military training institutions and schools with the IITs, namely the universities of the country, and to the exploitation of cumulative scientific knowledge and experience of thinking tanks, as well as with corresponding schools abroad, in international cooperation. Ladies and gentlemen, the policies of the Ministry of Defence will be at full cost and with the wider strategic government choices, as they have been codified on the individual points put before the Prime Minister. The Ministry supports and seeks synergies and coordinated initiatives with other ministries as part of the holistic approach to government work as a whole. For example, as regards the strategic choice of aid to Greece in a node that changes, for example, the Ministry aims to stimulate and strengthen defence diplomacy as a basic tool for soft power, to strengthen, of course, the role of the country's overall diplomacy. Already, officials from the Armed Forces record a strong presence at international organisations and bilateral and multilateral partnerships, particularly on the basis of the strategic cooperation relationship with Cyprus, Israel and Egypt, as well as other states in the wider region of the Balkans and the south-eastern Mediterranean. This scheme continues and is being strengthened. As regards the strategic choice of quality public health for all Greeks, the Ministry of National Defence will contribute to public health - already does - by optimising the services of military hospitals and by making military doctors available to social actions. A military doctor in the Acropolis was placed on the first day of taking up our duties. Emphasis will also be placed on the provision of health services in remote and remote areas, as well as on the immediate response to health crisis management. Yesterday, just once again, a military helicopter carried, that is, a patient from a remote island area to the center. Furthermore, with regard to the strategic choice of the value of the real estate, we aim to make best use of the assets of the Ministry of Defence by strengthening and simplifying the role of the National Defence Fund through the rational management of its assets. The aim is to increase the resources for the country's defence. In the same philosophy, the optimisation of the operation of the Armed Forces Assurance Agency is part of the same philosophy, so that the Ministry of Defence is, among other things, a development arm of the national effort. Finally, with regard to the strategic choice of environmental protection - adaptation to climate change and sustainable development, we aim to upgrade environmental policy through the emphasis on energy upgrading and autonomy of the Armed Forces infrastructure, rehabilitation of polluting forms of energy and the adoption of best practices in waste management. After all, ladies and gentlemen, it is well known that the Mission of the Armed Forces is not confined to its operational work alone. With a high sense of responsibility and sensitivity, however, the Armed Forces offer a multifaceted work toward society, based on links with the wider social groups. They take a large number of actions to help the rest of the state bodies, but also to link more closely to the society as a whole. As a government, we intend to substantially strengthen actions of direct cooperation with Civil Protection for its upgraded role, such as research and rescue, aerospace, fire and fire protection, the treatment of natural disasters at national level, in cooperation with the relevant ministries. Days ago, again in the early hours of taking up our duties, new rhythms and new efficiencies appeared when dealing with the theory in Halkidiki. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, the Armed Forces are next to society because their inspiring potential comes through society. So by making a reference to this inspiring potential, I would like to stress that we are very proud of it. Men and women of the Armed Forces operate with commitment, dignity, with an absolute sense of duty, professionally. The quality of our staff, after all, ensures this very deterrent power of the Armed Forces, and that is why it is our duty to stand next to the staff. To this end, we are planning a series of measures of a social nature to deal with issues relating to the daily lives of officials, but also with their families, living conditions and improving their standard of living, in a safe, modern and special, as well as in a working environment. At the same time, we owe special mention and attention to retired, stationary and ethnic guards. They are Greek patriots who serve or continue to serve with commitment and sense of responsibility the Armed Forces and Greece through them. This overall excellent attitude that the Armed Forces staff have shown, despite recent budgetary difficulties, is recognized, praised, and we are going to prove this respect for the personnel of the Armed Forces in the implementation of our programme. Finally, our top priority, in terms of what I have said, is transparency and accountability in the management of public money available to upgrade and modernise the Armed Forces. We want every Greek citizen to know where every euro is spent in the area of defence and this expenditure, wherever and whenever it is done, will be done with full respect for the lack of the Greek taxpayer. In conclusion, we aim to modern, powerful and remarkable Armed Forces with high deterrent power. We are taking a dynamic approach to the present so that we can shape the future in a better way, not as a pig, but as the creators of developments. The effort has always been continuous and intensive, just now it is becoming more intensive. Ladies and gentlemen, as it happens in every country that respects itself, but also faces modern security challenges, we have, as the political leadership of the Ministry of National Defence, the crucial, patriotic duty to care for a sword. The sword of Greece is its Armed Forces, it is "the cut of the sword or the terrible". And if in a time of peace this sword is in the dock, in troubled times, when the hand comes to the handle, then the sword must be sharp, sharp and shiny. I hope - I really hope - that the hand remains on the handle and the sword on the dock. We're not warlike. But we must not be unreserved. That is why I look like the rest of the ministry's political and military leadership, so that the sword of Greece, this sword, is in the situation that I described earlier. This is our job, this is our duty, to make every effort, with every pet of our forces, at all costs, to keep the cutting of the sword terrible. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-22-S1-commons.u140	Ladies and gentlemen, today's sitting is my first meeting. Above all, I feel obliged to thank the Prime Minister for the honour he has made me trusted with and entrusting me with the highest multipliers in society. I will honor his trust. [[At this point, the fourth Vice-President of Parliament, Mr DIMITRIOS VITSAS, takes over the Presidential Chair.]] Let me put our programme before you on welfare and social solidarity for the next few years, a programme resulting from the Prime Minister's commitments, but also from our vision of a fair and effective social policy, the main axes and measures needed to implement it, as well as our priorities for substantial changes in the bad texts of previous years, as well as being loyal to our deep conviction in the continuation of the state, strengthening the positive measures that we have taken to the Ministry from the previous administration. We describe our programming policy in a concise way with a horizontal and three vertical axes. The horizontal: our demographic problem. The three perpendiculars: First, strengthening the institution of the family. Secondly, development of a strategic social policy framework. Thirdly, independent living for people with disabilities. Our demographic problem: In recent years all research has shown that we have been faced with a painful reality. In our country every year fewer people are born than those who die. We have a hundred and twenty-five deaths for eighty-eight thousand births. According to the recent report by the Intergroup of Parliament's Committee on Employment and Social Affairs, the downward trend in the population is such that, in thirty-five years, our populations are at risk of shrinking in 1/4. We now need a national demographic strategy that goes beyond parties and policies. We want you all to be involved in strengthening the welfare and health system, social cohesion, pension and production potential, by developing the labour market. The demographic problem can only be tackled through a central governmental family policy. I'm going to the first vertical axis: I'm strengthening the institution of the family. The family continues to be a key cell of society and a strong social protection factor after the organized state. By aiming at our action in the institution of the family, we are moving on two axes, while taking qualitative and quantitative measures at the same time. Quality measures: We ensure that all children have positions in kindergartens. We support the programme 'Harmonisation of family and working life', so that not only can the available income increase in each family - and here comes the quality of this measure - but also to increase the participation of women, mothers in the labour market and to strengthen the socialisation of children at younger age, since all the research shows that the smaller the children are and are part of organised socialisation structures, the greater and more positive is the impact on the human capital and the potential of the country. Quantitative measures: We apply at least four social measures at the same time. Firstly, we introduce an allowance of EUR 2,000 for every child born in Greece. With this measure we face the increased needs of the first postnatal period, so that these costs do not constitute an implicating factor, especially for each new pair. Secondly, we are raising the tax limit by EUR 1,000 for each child. Thirdly, we support the school meal programme. Fourthly, we are considering the possibility of providing additional incentives specifically for younger families, those that women give birth 30 years ago, since we know that the younger the woman, the higher the possibility of giving birth to a child. Second vertical axis: development of a strategic social policy framework. The country's social policy framework has had a key feature for decades: relatively high social costs as a percentage of GDP, but a low impact on poverty rates. According to ELSTAT and EUROSTAT data, between 1995 and 2008 expenditure on social protection shifted from 20% to 26% of GDP, thus reaching the average of the European Union and at the same time the rate of absolute poverty remained unchanged. In other words, the State carried out satisfactory quantitative expenditure, but it was unable to strengthen, relieve the most vulnerable. The introduction in 2014 of the minimum guaranteed income by the Government of New Democracy has decisively faced these distortions of the social protection system. Unfortunately, the previous government, although smuggled it as a trap of poverty, initially suspending - if we all remember - for two years its universal geographical application, not only accepted its correctness, but attempted and credited it politically as a so-called institutional child. Even for its implementation, however, it could hardly be proud. We can all remember the images of a total lack of organisation, terrible suffering, great tails of all the beneficiaries who joined the municipalities' services to submit their requests. The minimum guaranteed income in 2014 was the beginning of our attempt at social policy expenditure with clear targeting and tangible results. That is why we are strengthening it so that the appropriations entered in the 2020 budget are EUR 1 billion from EUR 850,000 000 000, which is today. Also - something very important for its absorption - we seek to target the most vulnerable population groups, so that the sections of the population close to the limit of absolute poverty, such as single-parent families and families with minors, are given the help they need. I quickly pass on the third vertical axis, the independent living of people with disabilities. Our aim is to do the independent living and accessibility principle, fundamental human rights enshrined in Articles 19 and 9 of the United Nations Convention. So we have three main policies. Firstly, the regulatory safeguarding of access for disabled people to the labour market and high-responsibility positions. Secondly, the upgrading of their special education in cooperation with the Ministry of Education. Thirdly, establishing a staff qualification with full professional and insurance rights. These are the main axes of our programming policy. Before I close, you'll let me make a little mention of the bodies supervised by our department. Already in the first days of taking up our duties, we have received a number of requests for intervention from the Ministry in order to solve pressing problems even as regards the basics for the operation of the supervised social welfare institutions, such as the payment of workers and the housing of their services. It is understandable that the budgetary constraints of recent years have had an impact on the functioning of supervised bodies. But what is not understood, given this restrictive financial framework, is that the previous political leadership has not even attempted to solve problems of an organisational nature of the bodies, which are not just burdening them, but often leading to their paralysis. We know that the challenge is great and difficult. However, as every pessimistic finds the difficulty at every opportunity, every optimist finds the opportunity at every difficulty. Because we are also optimistic, but above all very hardworking, you have no doubt that we will do the job and do it properly. Thank you.

Battered are the words 'Yugoslavia', 'Afghanistan', 'Iraq', 'Libya', 'Syria' and others. The pretexts for their interventions are known and overshadowed. Energy security, cyber-war, the supposed restoration of democracy and so forth, are invoking counter-terrorism. Who? But those who created, financed and trained terrorist organisations like ISIS, Al-Qaida and others. Didn't you know? Does the Minister of Foreign Affairs not know about this when he scams on his successful meetings with the red tape of the US Department of Foreign Affairs? The European Union, as a transnational imperialist alliance, is diversely involved in intra-imperialist competition. Strong Member States - its members actively participate in the wars in the Syrian, Libyan, sub-Saharan Africa. The European Union is NATO's close ally with its cooperation to be strengthened in recent years. Although the European Union is facing NATO as a key pillar in the security of its capitalist member states, it is gradually preparing its Eurocrat. That is why it makes use of agreements such as PESCO, to which Greece, by decision of the previous government of SYRIZA, participates. The involvement of our country in these imperialist plans - direct and indirect - is undeniable. Unfortunately the result of this involvement is also the orientation of the doctrine of the use of the Greek Armed Forces. When Greek foreign policy is geared to supporting US projects, NATO and the European Union, then the Armed Forces of our country, a key pillar in supporting its foreign policy, are also oriented towards serving these projects. This is an extremely dangerous option. And it is dangerous for our people, of course, because the result of this involvement is the forced division of the power of the Greek Armed Forces into two axes. One is intended to support imperialists. The other, which is dramatically downgraded, is about the possibility of defending our borders, our sovereign rights. And this is happening today where the top of Turkish aggression is dominant. Moreover, even the first-year students of military productive schools know that from the analysis of the doctrine of the use of the Armed Forces, their missions, the necessary weapon systems are identified, i.e. armaments and personnel training. So these are adapted to imperialist needs. This is why precious resources of the chapter “House Forces” are wasted in unnecessarily outdated areas, Finland, Libya and elsewhere. It is extremely dangerous for the people, the workers, the unemployed, the youth, the New Democracy's intention to incorporate into the Armed Forces' missions and to provide security at home, in the city and in the country, that is to say, the Armed Forces and police duties. This design is the result of the Namibian directive on the treatment of asymmetric threats, a directive which directly targets the people and their struggle. These positions must be condemned by the people and also by the personnel of the Armed Forces. The majority of this staff come from people's families, victims, and yet their anti-popular policy of PASOK, SYRIZA and New Democracy. Their interests are common to workers, poor farmers, pensioners. There is no interest in our country participating in the US, NATO, the European Union and other imperialist alliances. The Communist Party of Greece will continue, strengthening its struggle, against our country's involvement in the imperialist plans to develop the common fight of the peoples against the imperialist wars. It will continue uninterruptedly to claim the satisfaction of the legitimate demands of the personnel of the Armed Forces. It will stand on the side of the resistance to the anti-popular attack on modern folk needs. Finally, the power of the Communist Party of Greece expresses the only philosophical way out, the struggle to become the people owner of wealth that produces socialism for the labour power.

[[The President-in-Office of Parliament, Mr KONSTADINOS TASOULAS, takes over the President-in-Office of the Council.]] I will begin with the issues of environment and planning, which I must say I will not be as detailed as I would wish, precisely because of the limitation of time. Anyway, I'll try to do the best. As a Ministry, our central axis is, of course, turning to a model of sustainable development. To this end, we are adopting a holistic approach that will enable us to contribute to tackling climate change - we would be elsewhere as a government if we did not see this issue - and to catch up to the objectives of the European Union and of course the Paris Agreement. We pay particular attention to green investment, to the promotion of eco-innovation and to the creation of many thousands of new quality jobs in this sector, because it is time to realise that environmental protection can become a catalyst for growth rather than an obstacle. In fact, some of the major projects to which the Prime Minister referred relate largely to our ministry. At the same time, we are promoting the transition to the circular economy, namely a model of production and consumption based on the reuse and recycling of materials. This shift beyond the positive impact on the environment, we know from recent studies that it can create sixteen thousand jobs in Greece in the near future. In this context, unfortunately, we know that in recycling our country's performance was and remain disappointing. Despite the previous government's announcements, recycling has been stuck in the past four years at 18%, while the Community target is 50% of the year and the Community average is now 46%. I think we are only over two other Community countries. The necessary step to reverse this situation is to promote intensively the sorting at source in separate currents, starting with the promotion of coffee bins, as most environmental organisations, namely the bins for organic waste, are supporting, which we will pay particular attention to. I want to focus on the plastic pollution that particularly affects our seas. As the Prime Minister has said, Greece has the possibility of becoming a pioneer in the Mediterranean in the battle against plastics, and we will pursue this by adopting the relevant European directive. The Prime Minister's reference to the abolition of disposable plastics by 2021 is clear. We also know that an issue of the utmost importance is integrated waste management, focusing particularly on public-private partnerships in cooperation with the Ministry of Development. As Minister, I had personally contributed to such initiatives, such as in Epirus, Serres, Elias, in western Macedonia. I am really sorry that the former District Minister of Attica has canceled projects in the largest region of the country, and so now in Epirus, for example, or in western Macedonia, the issue has been resolved, while in Attica we are threatened with garbage hills. In this same subject we are considering the application of efficient energy techniques to recover energy from waste, so that we are at the forefront of innovation, adopting good practices from countries such as Finland, for example, because there are ideas in our country even today that ignore what is happening in the other advanced European countries. One issue we are focusing particularly on is the major delays in environmental permits that are an important obstacle to attracting investment, including renewable energy sources. We have prepared a bill on this, which I assume will be incorporated into the government's broader development bill on simplifying and speeding up procedures under the relevant European directive. At the same time, we are rapidly promoting the completion of the register, for which, ladies and gentlemen of the SYRIZA, I recognise that progress has been made. We also pay particular attention to the protected areas, particularly the NATURA areas, which can be models of sustainable development. In the forests we focus on the completion of forest maps, for which steps have been taken towardswards, speeding up opposition-solving processes and setting out the specifications for modern anti-nuclear designs. In our spatial planning and town planning, the main objective is sustainable spatial development. We are launching the immediate dismantling of flagial investments that are unduly delaying, such as the Greek one. We are moving towards a radical simplification of the legislation and the planning and licensing procedures and defining land uses throughout the country, so that everyone knows where to conduct a specific activity with legal certainty. We're not staying on some theoretical announcements. To this end, we will implement a programme for the development of local spatial projects, in other words, at municipal level, aimed at covering more than 70% of land use at the end of the four-year period. I move on with the issues of international energy policy at the top of the agenda of the Ministry of the Environment and Energy. Our main aim is to optimise the country's energy security, as today we have full dependence on imported hydrocarbons, which cover more than 2/3 of our energy balance. I repeat they are imported hydrocarbons. We will change this with the infrastructure and the assistance of our energy partners to maximise the diversification of sources and routes in the import of hydrocarbons beyond the development of renewable energy sources, which we will also pursue dynamically. This includes the completion of the IGB and its long-term IGB, the connector, i.e. with Bulgaria, the promotion of new infrastructure for the introduction of LNG, liquefied gas, private investments such as the so-called FSRU in Alexandroupolis and the underwater gas tank in Kavala, the promotion of IGI Poseidon and the implementation of the East Med pipeline, in addition to the consolidation of our strategy of cooperation with our traditional energy importers. We are further deepening our strategic synergies towards south-eastern and paradoxical Europe, with emphasis on four-way cooperation with Serbia, Romania and Bulgaria, and on the other hand towards the south-eastern Mediterranean and further regional cooperation at institutional level with Cyprus, Israel and Egypt. I will be in Cairo tomorrow, in the presence of the American Energy Minister, to discuss exactly these issues. For us energy is also foreign policy, as we seek Greece to become a pillar of stability, security and prosperity within an unstable region. Ladies and gentlemen, I now come to the most urgent issue, which is, of course, PPC's rescue plan. The government of SYRIZA - ANEL reached the company to the limits of the collapse. ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-22-S1-commons.u266	The Minister of State Mr George Gerapetritis has the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2019-07-22-S1-commons.u267	Thank you very much, Mr President, and I would like to thank the House. I wanted to point out in my first speech as a member of the new government that I feel particularly honored and thrilled to be in this sacred area of democracy and to point out that the purpose of current governance will be to upgrade the institutions as a whole so that democracy can find a modern footprint. To highlight from the outset the fact that current governance has acted in a way that has no precedent. The speed at which the state political mechanism was carried out, the speed at which the necessary decrees and decisions were adopted, so that the administration can function on the first day, is a unique phenomenon in Migration. In fifteen days, actions were completed, which took six or more months in previous governments. And, of course, I should like to point out that there is an absolute readiness and maturity so that the necessary legislative texts can be tabled so that the country can function. Already before the closure of the Parliament, which is scheduled for 10 August, three bills will be submitted, concerning the final state, the tax administration and arrangements concerning the local government agencies and other urgent provisions, so that this institutional reopening can take place. The first, in particular, bill, which symbolically chose the current government to be about the organization of the state and the state, is a piece of legislation that did not exist, was missing from Greek public life. For the first time an overall governance legislation is introduced, a governance manual, which is based on three axes. These are: we change the government, we trust the administration and we update the House as a whole. As far as the government is concerned, we're changing the management model as a whole. Unfortunately, the Ministerial Council, as it did in Post-Population, was nothing different from a collective instrument for legitimising previous decisions. It was not the constitutional role it has played and entrusted to it, which is the production of public policies. It will therefore be restored to the nature of the Ministerial Council as a key policy forum by collective procedures, by means of an organisational plan, which will operate within the government. And this programme, which will be produced by the Ministerial Council and which will function at regular intervals and will have a specific mandate each time, will be monitored by a central system of governance, so that any project undergoes can be monitored at any time, to see if there are any delays and to have the necessary coordination of the government work. These are innovative tools, which in foreign legal classes, advanced, are self-evident, but unfortunately in Greece they have been delayed for decades. Of course, for the first time - and this is one of the paradoxes of Greek political life - they will be introduced at the level of the Minister, the Deputy Secretary-General, the Secretary-General as a whole, a web of rules of impartiality so that all these political positions will be blocked and incompatible at the time the post is held and proactively, after the end of the government service. We trust the administration! Ladies and gentlemen, I have listened with interest to what has been said by the opposition about the administration's politicisation and partyisation operation. With all due respect, you will allow me to say that the exact opposite is true. And that I reserve to demonstrate by depositing the draft law on the final state. The first question was raised in connection with the alleged abolition of the secretaries-general. Law No.4369/2016, a law brought about by previous governance, provided for a procedure, which basically brought the AACC to propose three candidates and, ultimately, the Minister chose the Secretary-General. What was the fate of this process? A single Secretary-General was appointed for three years. Who was that secretary General? The one who previously held the post as a appointed Secretary-General. A system that failed. Instead, the final state will have a new arrangement, which introduces a uniform degree of secretary-general, that is to say a secretary from the administration itself, a public career employee, who will have all the administration, the economy and coordination and will come under the procedures of the IASC, working procedures provided for by the Boards of Directors under an impartial procedure and will, of course, be subject to the Public Service Code. This is a complete depopulation and this - you will allow me to say that - in my humble opinion is the greatest innovation, which will come to Greek public life, as a critical mass of administrative material is transferred from political leadership to service administration. How's that gonna happen? It will be done as follows: In principle, all administrative acts of a specific nature, which so far have reached the level of the Minister or the State Secretary, will now be adopted as a rule at the level of the administration. In other words, the Director-General, who comes from the administration, who will express the continuity of the administration and will not be subject to changes from any political treaty, will finally decide on the adoption of the act by his signature. Imagine what economy this means from the point of view of the bureaucracy and the time that it will take. The decisions will be taken by the administration. We trust publicity. I have heard with real and genuine questions that we are supposed to abolish control mechanisms, i.e. the Economic Crime Corps, the Labour Inspectors' College and the Environment and Energy Inspectors' Corps. I wondered why he was completely untrue. And I was amazed to hear this because this is precisely the follow-up to what the previous government started, which was governed by Law No.4336/2015, then the current government itself, with the agreement of the then opposition and current government majority, transferred all tax controls from the political leadership to the tax administration. What he has done at that time, we are extending it today as follows: Instead of all the control bodies being subject to a political chief, who is the Special Secretary or the Secretary-General, he will instead be subject to the management of careers, to the Director-General. All the audit bodies will no longer receive a political mandate, but will only have an administrative hierarchy. That's the genuine demilitarization. Of course, we are introducing a system which is very strong, in the standards of the English administrative system, with a single transparency authority, which controls the entire administration with guarantees of full independence and impartiality. Finally, we are introducing a prospect of upgrading the House. Ladies and gentlemen, my own short story, particularly academic, is based on the support of the representative system and Parliament. It is what I have dealt with for twenty years and for which I feel I am accountable to both society and my students. It's a personal bet. I'm the one with my favourite channels. I've got Parliament's channel. I am one of the few who watched it, and today I will have the honour of being part of this process, but it is our bet that the upgrading of the House and I already feel that we have taken a step. At the Prime Minister's initiative, the parliamentary practice of all post-population has changed. On the first day of the Programming Declarations, instead of having a sterile reading of the Programming Declarations by the Prime Minister, what did we have? We had a genuine extensive dialogue, which was the Prime Minister and the entire opposition. But he did so consciously because this consultation promotes parliamentary work. In addition, what is true is that we will fight to the extent that we are allowed to combat this pathology, what we call'major parliamentaryism'. We will never come to Parliament to say that we are right, because we are more. We will not. We will come to give rights to opposition and will be able through this productive dialogue to reach the best legislature. Our writing samples are very specific. We have proposed in the Constitutional Review - and it has been widely accepted - that the committees of inquiry can also be convened by the opposition individually. This is a great concession, which comes from our conscious choice. And, of course, what is true is that there will be a wide-ranging grid of codifications and simplifications of existing legislation and very strict rules for good legislature. All this will be reflected in the constitutional review process, which is due to begin shortly, because our conviction is that there should be, even in the limited context set by the proposed Parliament, a very substantial constitutional revision. We will not accept the phenomena of a constitutional express review, as happened in the previous House, with only eighteen meetings of the parliamentary committee and two meetings in plenary. We will, hopefully, have a full consultation. Finally, Mr President, allow me one final reference to the ancient Greek secretariat. Plutarchus said that there are two categories of people involved in public life: there are demagogues and politicians. It is the demagogues who are addressing those who will vote in the next election and may put measures only to mock their ears a few days before the elections. And there are politicians who think and express, not only today's generation but also next generations. I would like to assure you, as far as I am concerned, that we will be the voice of today, but above all of the next generations in our opinion and vote. Thank you very much.

I stress at this point that we are discussing an extension of the museum without relocation of the Archaeological School. Besides, the proximity of the two institutions is an advantage. And for this particular project, we will work with the Republican authorities. The demolition of Korydallos prisons, in order to transform the area into a model altar, a breath for the entire region. I have already given directions to the Ministry of Citizen Protection to seek land within Attica, near the main roads, in which a new prison can be built, which will also welcome the prisoners of Korydallos. The development of the former Tatoi palaces as an area of culture, tourism and sport. The transformation of the old Drapetsona fertilizers into a green park and innovation. Finally, the overall upgrade of the Thessaloniki International Exhibition, so that Thessaloniki will become a commercial, cultural, economic centre throughout the year. Ladies and gentlemen, with an active part in this three-day process I have seen - I imagine many others have found it - a relative discomfort in terms of counter-population. The honourable Members have raised a number of questions and it is quite reasonable to ask questions. I believe that both the ministers and I personally responded to what we could. Besides, don't worry. I intend to make use of the Prime Minister's time so that I can answer questions asked by the political leaders, but also by individual Members. I do not have the time to answer all the additional questions raised by the political leaders in their dentistry. However, I would like to focus on a specific issue, which concerns the Public Power Corporation. Mr. Tsipras, when you received PPC, it had a capitalisation of 1.7 billion. You handed it over by capitalisation, which was a little more than 300 million. And because people will rush around to say, like Mr. Tsakalotos or Mr. Varoufakis, that the capitalisation of a company is not necessarily an indication of its economic viability, of course I do not know many companies lose 80% of their capitalisation if they go brightly...
